[trialAccount t18311201-282]
NEW COURT. THURSDAY, DECEMBER 8.
Fourth Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
282. JAMES WOOD was indicted for stealing, on the 2nd of December, 1 half-crown, the money of William Williamson.
WILLIAM WILLIAMSON. I live in Brown's-lane, Spitalfields, and am a trimming-maker. I have employed the prisoner between two and three years; he slept in the house on the 2nd of December - I had counted my money that night; I had one sovereign and five half-crowns in my watch-pocket, and one shilling and some halfpence in my trousers pocket, on a chair by the side of my bed - I awoke about half-past six o'clock; he was out of bed, and I heard the money rattle - I laid very still for a few minutes, and then I saw him go down on his knees, and replace the trousers on the chair; he left the room - I counted the money, and sent for a Policeman.
WILLIAM DRABBLE. I am a Police-officer. I was sent for to the prosecutor's - I received the prisoner in charge, and found on him three 1d. pieces, a farthing, a knife, and a pair of scissors, but no half-crown.
WILLIAM WILLIAMSON. I told him I had sent for an officer; he had about an hour to get rid of the money before he came - he was not in my sight all that time; I never found any thing wrong in him before.
GUILTY. Aged 20.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18311201-283]
283. DANIEL MARCHANT was indicted for stealing, on the 2nd of December, 3 half-crowns, 5 shillings, 1 sixpence, and 11 1/2d. in copper monies, the monies of Joseph Allen, from his person.
JOSEPH ALLEN. I am a gardener, and live in Lissongrove. On the afternoon of the 2nd of December, I was in the Champion public-house, in Earl-street, Lisson-grove; I had some bread and meat, which I laid down - I counted my money before I went in, as I had been taking up some trees, and did not know exactly what I had; I found I had 13s. in silver in my right-hand coat pocket, and 11 1/2d. in my left-hand coat pocket - I put three half-crowns in my breast-pocket, leaving the other, which was in sixpences and shillings, in my coat pocket; I went into the tap-room, had a pint of beer, and saw the prisoner sitting in another box by the fire - I was rather fresh with liquor; I fell into a dose - I was not exactly fast asleep; I felt the prisoner's hand in my pocket - I took hold of it; he ran across into the box where he came from; I charged him with picking my pocket, as I found I had not one farthing left, and told him I would serve him out for it - I went to the door, sent a man for a Policeman, and staid outside the door a quarter of an hour or twenty minutes, while he went for him.
Cross-examined by MR. J. ALLEY. Q.When are you paid your wages? A. At uncertain times; I had then been at work for Mrs. Lewis, North-bank - I had taken 24s.; I was not very drunk: when I get a little liquor it takes my head, and I have been desired by publicans to stay away from their houses; I had another pint of porter at Maida-hill, and one at the Portman Arms, in the afternoon - I had had no ale nor spirits that day, and I had one pint at the Champion; I carried my silver in my coat pocket, because both my trousers pockets were torn - I went in behind the door of the Champion; I did not see any one but the prisoner - he sat between two women in a box in a corner; there might be other persons in the room; I sat by myself - there was no one in the next box- I did not want any man in the room to fight, that I will swear; I was not exactly asleep - I should have been asleep if I had not been disturbed; the prisoner's hand was in my pocket, and I caught hold of it - he twisted it from me, and went back to his seat; I told him I would serve him out - I went out in a moment for the Policeman; I do not know how many persons were there - there were several persons in the room when the Policeman came; I was very much agitated - I sent an Irish labourer for a Policeman; he was a stranger to me - I gave him a shilling yesterday morning - the prisoner did not attempt to run away, but staid in the room between the two women - I have charged several persons with robbing me; I never took any body up - I have been robbed there before.
COURT. Q. How many persons were in the room when you went out for an officer? A. I did not take any notice - the prisoner and the women were there; there might be other men.
STEPHEN HAYWARD. I am a Police-officer. I wentto the Champion public-house, in Earl-street; the prosecutor stood at the door - he said there was a man inside who had picked his pocket; he appeared very fresh - he did not appear as if he could know if a person's hand was in his pocket - he did not appear able to take care of himself; I took the prisoner to the watch-house, and there searched him - there might be seven or eight persons in the tap-room; I did not search the prisoner till I got to Harcourt-street, which is half a mile from the house; I found on him a knife, a key, and 2 1/2d. - I do not think the prosecutor could tell whether he had hold of a man's arm or not.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18311201-284]
284. WILLIAM DAVIS and JOHN HUMPHREYS were indicted for stealing, on the 1st of December, 1 stove, value 9s., the goods of Thomas Read.
THOMAS READ. I live in Thomas-street, Brick-lane, and am a broker. On the 1st of December I missed a stove from outside my shop - I found it at a broker's in Club-row next day.
ANN GILL. I live in Club-row, Bethnal-green, and am a broker. On the 1st of December Davis came to my shop, and asked if I would go and look at a stove - he said he lived in Phoenix-street; I got my bonnet, and went with him - it was standing against the wall inside, Humphreys was sitting by the fire; he turned round and said, "The reason we want to part with it is, the other is fixed" - they asked 5s.; I offered 4s.; they said "No, 4s. 6d." - I was going out, and Davis said, I should have it; I said, "You must bring it home," which Davis did - it was put out the next day, and Mr. Read came and claimed it; I went with him, and the officer - I pointed out to them where the prisoners were, and they were both at home.
JOHN GREEN. I am a Police-officer. I was applied to by the prosecutor, and took the prisoners.
Humphreys' Defence. I was standing at my mother's door in Phoenix-street; a man came and rested this stove by the side of the door, and Davis asked if he had it to sell; we bought it for 3s.
The prisoners received good characters.
DAVIS - GUILTY. Aged 16.
HUMPHREYS - GUILTY. Aged 16.
Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18311201-285]
285. JOHN HOPKINS was indicted for stealing, on the 30th of November, 3 spoons, value 20s., the goods of William Lawrence.
WILLIAM LAWRENCE. I keep Serle's coffee-house, Carey-street, Lincon's Inn-fields . On the evening of the 30th of November, the prisoner came into the coffee-room about a quarter before six o'clock; he staid there ten minutes or a quarter of an hour, had a glass of ale, and was going out, when I heard a sound of silver; I waited at the door, and allowed him to pass me, and saw obtruding from his right-hand coat pocket, a spoon or prong; I followed him: he began to run, and run towards Bell-yard - I came up and caught him by the collar; I heard some silver fall, and all the observation he made was, "I have no plate."
Cross-examined by MR. STURGEON. Q. You are not certain whether it was a spoon or a fork? A. No - he did not appear intoxicated.
JOHN WARREN KEETLEY. I live at Derby, and am a conveyancer. On the 30th of November I was sitting in the coffee-room, and saw a person walk across the room from the adjoining box, towards the door; he went about a yard past the table, in the centre of the room, which is used as a sideboard - he then took a number of spoons from the sideboard, went towards the door, and was putting them into his pocket - I was rising from my seat, and at that moment Mr. Lawrence opened the half door; the man ran out at the other door - two of the spoons fell from his hand or his pocket.
Cross-examined. Q. You do not know that the prisoner is the man? A. No.
JOHN WEEKS. I am waiter to Mr. Lawrence. I saw the prisoner in my master's coffee-room - he had a glass of ale, and then asked me to give him another - I left the room to do so; there were some silver spoons on the centre table of the room - when I returned with the ale I picked up two spoons at the door; the prisoner was then just gone out, and my master after him - I took the two spoons to the table, and found that one was then missing.
Cross-examined. Q. Are you sure there were no forks among them? A. Not among the spoons; there were at another part of the table - he did not appear intoxicated.
MR. LAWRENCE. I saw the prisoner stopped, without losing sight of him; the third spoon has not been found - these are the two.
Prisoner's Defence. I was going out, but finding I had left my gloves upon the table I went back, and called for another glass of ale; finding I had had enough before I went out without it - I made a stumble, and pushed the spoons from the table on the floor.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 21. - Confined Three Weeks.
[trialAccount t18311201-286]
286. MARY COSGROVE was indicted for stealing, on the 29th of November, 10 lbs. of bacon, value 5s., the goods of James Battram.
The prosecutor's name being John Battram, the prisoner was ACQUITTED.
[trialAccount t18311201-287]
287. THOMAS CORMACK was indicted for stealing, on the 26th of November, 1 iron trevet, value 4s., the goods of Thomas Bennett.
THOMAS BENNETT. I am a journeyman weaver, and live in Brick-lane, Spitalfields - the prisoner is my cousin. On the 26th of November he came and drank tea with me and my wife; we went to wind some quills at the other end of the shop - the prisoner was then leaning against my loom-post; he then went down stairs, and I went to my loom and missed a trevet, with which we cut the plush; I afterwards saw him in custody,
ELIZABETH BENNETT. I was present while the prisoner had tea - we missed the trevet before he was down the second pair of stairs; I saw him in Spital-square on the Tuesday, and gave him into custody.
ARTHUR SOWERBY. I live in Wheeler-street, Spitalfields - I assist my brother, who is a pawnbroker. I have a trevet, pawned on the 26th of November, between six and seven o'clock in the evening, by the prisoner, for 2s.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 25.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Fourteen Days.
[trialAccount t18311201-288]
288. GEORGE BLOOMFIELD was indicted for stealing, on the 2nd of December, 1 boot, value 3s., the goods of Jonathan Fimister.
THOMAS CHAMBERS. I am in the employ of Jonathan Fimister, of No. 48, Broad-street, Ratcliff-highway, a shoemaker. I was standing in a room at the bottom of the shop on the morning of the 2nd of December; I heard a boot fall from the door - I went into Stepney-causeway, and saw a boy running; I called to Andrew Price, and told him.
ANDREW PRICE. I am a shoemaker, in the employ of Fimister. Chambers met me as I was going to the shop- I followed the prisoner; he ran towards Brook-street, and I pursued him, crying Stop thief! in a short time I saw him take a boot from under his coat, and throw it among some rubbish - the officer pursued, and took him in the Commercial-road; I took up this boot, and followed him - he was taken in two minutes afterwards; this is my master's.
JOHN ROBINSON. I am an officer. I saw the prisoner running, and I took him; he said he did not take the boot, it was another boy.
GUILTY. Aged 16. - Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18311201-289]
289. ELLEN BROOKS was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of November, 1 bonnet, value 2s. 6d., and 1 shawl, value 2s., the goods of Ann Williams.
ANN WILLIAMS. I am an unfortunate girl, and live in New Gravel-lane. I met the prisoner in Ratcliff-highway on the 28th of November; I knew her before; she said she had no home - I took her to my home, and gave her a night's lodging - she was very much intoxicated, and said she had run out of dress lodgings, meaning a Jews, who keeps girls to go upon the town; I had only 1s. - I went and got a lodging for her and myself that night, and then took her to my own lodging, where she remained till the following Monday; I was very poorly that day, and did not get up till a quarter past two o'clock, when I went down stairs, and when I came up I missed my bonnet and shawl, and she was gone - there was no other person in the room. I afterwards went to No. 30, Albion-street, and while I was there, she came in; I said "There you are;" she made no answer; she had my bonnet on her head - I got the officer.
Prisoner. She lent me her bonnet, and I left my black hat there. Witness. The black hat was the one she ran out of the dress lodgings in, and the Jewess came and had that away - I had lent her my bonnet the day before, but not that day; my shawl was India crape, worth about 2s. - a woman in Albion-street knew me, and told me that Ellen was at No. 30 there; that was on the Thursday after she left me.
THOMAS BATES. I am a Police-officer. I was called to take the prisoner - I took this bonnet from her head; she denied all knowledge of the parties - I then asked what she had done with the black bonnet she used to wear she said she left it in exchange.
Prisoner's Defence. She lent me the bonnet to go out for some hair-pins; the shawl belongs to the persons I had been living with.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
[trialAccount t18311201-290]
290. ELLEN BROOKS was again indicted for stealing, on the 24th of November, 1 gown, value 12s., the goods of Sarah Medcraft.
SARAH MEDCRAFT. I live at a house, kept by a person named Manuel, in Cooper's-court, near the London-docks. I had not known the prisoner till last Thursday week, when she came to inquire for a lodging; I came down, and saw her sitting in the room - I afterwards lost a gown, which I saw at the pawnbroker's on the Saturday following.
JAMES FINCH. I am in the employ of a pawnbroker, in East Smithfield. I produce a gown, pawned by the prisoner, on the 24th of November, for 4s.
MARY MANUEL. I am the wife of John Manuel - we live in Cooper's-court. The prisoner came to my house, and asked for a lodging; I asked her to sit down, and have a cup of coffee, as I was at breakfast - she took it, and then she sent for a drop of wine; she then went up stairs - we got talking together; she said she lived in Tooley-street, and that she had got up very early - she said she paid 7s. or 7s. 6d. a week; I came down to get her some water - she came down in ten minutes, and said she would go and get more wine: she had 1s., and asked what it would come to - I said 10d.; she said she would go for it, and went out with a bottle, and never returned; I went up an hour or an hour and a half afterwards, and missed this gown from the room in which we had been: she had looked at it; she said it was a pretty thing, and asked if it was mine - I said No, it belonged to one of my lodgers.
Prisoner. I lent her a gown of mine - she returned it all worn out. Witness. She did lend me an old stuff gown once, and I returned it.
GUILTY. Aged 19. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18311201-291]
291. FRANCIS WELCH was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of October, 2 shirts, value 14s., the goods of William Osterbury.
WILLIAM OSTERBURY. I lost two shirts, on the 25th of October, out of a tub of water in the kitchen of the Swedish Flag, at St. George's in the East; I was not at home when they were taken.
MARY ANN LAY. I am servant at the Swedish Flag, in Princes-street. I put the two shirts into the tub about nine o'clock in the evening in quetion; I met the prisoner coming in from the yard - the shirts were in the kitchen, which communicates with the yard; he came in, and passed me - I went out, and had suspicions; I came in, and missed the shirts about half an hour after I had seen the prisoner; he was then gone - no other person had gone into the yard from the time I put the shirts into the tub till I missed them.
PETER PULMAN. I live at a pawnbroker's. We received some information on the 26th of October, and on the 28th a woman came into our shop to pawn one shirt, marked W. O. 3-31: in consequence of some questions my master put to her, the prisoner came in, and said the shirt belonged to him - I went and got a Policeman; on my return the prisoner was gone.
JOHN FLASHAM. I am a Police-officer. I produce the shirt, which I received in the presence of this witness.
MICHAEL FLENESEY. I am an officer. I took the prisoner, at No. 13, Dock-street; I knocked at the door - no one opened it or answered; I got a key and opened it, but the prisoner fastened it inside - I got in at last; the prisoner made some resistance, and said he was not the person I wanted.(Property produced and sworn to)
Prisoner. I met a young girl, and we went into the prosecutor's to have a pint of porter; I never was in there before, and know nothing about taking the shirts - I met the mother of the girl, and a man who used to work in the West India-docks; he said, "I will pay the price of a pot of porter," and he said to the woman, "Take this shirt and get the price of a peck of coals" - she went in, and we went to see why she stopped to long.
PETER PULMAN. The prisoner said it belonged to him, and the woman said it belonged to Frank Welch.
Prisoner's Defence. I never opened my lips, and never saw it till I saw it on the counter.
GUILTY. Aged 52 - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18311201-292]
Second London Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
292. WILLIAM PURDUE was indicted for a misdemeanor.
SARAH MAGGS. I assist at Mr. Burnell's pastry-cook's shop, in Fetter-lane. On the 26th of October the prisoner came in, and asked me to give him change for a 5s. piece - I said I could not; he said there was a pawnbrokers where he could get change by having it in halfpence - I told him to go to the pork-shop next door; he said he had come from there - I then gave him change in halfpence, and he gave me the 5s. piece - I looked at it, and called to a young man who was down stairs; the prisoner seeing him come up ran away - he was afterwards brought back; I am quite sure he is the same person - I gave the 5s. piece to Thomas Allen, who gave it to the officer; this is it.
Prisoner. Q. Will you positively swear I am the person? A. Yes; I knew you by your features when you were brought back; I knew you directly.
HENRY PRIDHAM. I was at Mr. Burnell's and was called up into the shop; I saw a man in the shop - he ran out, and I ran after him up the court; he fell down, and a paper of halfpence fell out of his hand and broke - he got up and run away leaving them on the ground - I saw Allen run after him; he was brought back - I had lost sight of him.
THOMAS LIGHTFOOT. I am an officer. I received the prisoner in custody - I have a crown-piece, which was delivered to me, in the presence of Maggs.
Prisoner. It was marked after I was brought back.
SARAH MAGGS. I had observed this mark on it before, but I afterwards made another - they are both on it.
Prisoner's Defence. I was in Farringdon-street; a baker came up, and said he wanted me - I asked for what - he said, "You have been to my master's, and offered a bad 5s. piece;" I can positively state that I am not the person - the prosecutrix might be deceived; there are nineteen butchers out of twenty who wear smock-frocks and red caps like mine, and that was what she swore to me by.
SARAH MAGGS. I did not speak of his dress, but of his person - I have never felt the least doubt of his being the man.
THOMAS LIGHTFOOT. I gave notice of this to the solicitor of the Mint; he said they would not interfere in a single uttering, unless there were two within a short time- I paid the expences of the prosecution myself; the solicitor of the Mint directed me to retain the coin, and put the date on it.
The Court then directed the crown-piece to be broken, which was done, and was evidently counterfeit.
GUILTY. Aged 22. - Confined Six Months, and to find sureties.
[trialAccount t18311201-293]
293. WILLIAM SPENCER was indicted for a misdemeanor.
JOHN NEWMAN CRAWFORD. I live in Cheapside, and am an umbrella-maker. On the evening of the 11th of November the prisoner came into my shop, and asked for a sixpenny stick - he threw down a counterfeit 5s. piece to my wife; I was standing behind a desk, where he could not see me: I went forward, took up the crown-piece, and said, "I don't like this, my friend, have you a sixpence;" he said No: I took it to a vice in the shop, and tried it - I found it was bad; he said, "You must not deface it, I will take it back" - I said, "You shall not take that back, nor yourself;" he then dropped the stick and ran off - I pursued him; as he found I was gaiming ground, he stopped at the corner of Friday-street, and said "There he goes" but I took him; I am certain he is the person - I had not lost sight of him. Mr. Powell, the solicitor of the Mint, said they would attend to it, but I heard no more of them, and I was forced to go before the Grand Jury - I there saw Mr. Powell and Mr. Field, who said the Mint did not think it worth their while to prosecute unless there was a second uttering.
THOMAS DAVIS. I took the prisoner, and produce the crown-piece which I received from the prosecutor.
The prisoner read a long Defence, stating that he had been employed as a Law-writer , and had received the crown-piece with some other money, of a Mr. Jones , at Williams' coffee-house - he went to buy the stick, and took the crown-piece out by accident to pay for it; that when the prosecutor refused it he had run out to find the gentleman he took it from and the prosecutor pursued and took him .
The crown-piece being broken was evidently counterfeit .
GUILTY. Aged 35.
Confined Six Months, and to find Sureties.
